## 17 Nov 2026 — Official VM one-shot architecture and automatic delete (source-only decision)

**New official evidence (no live Cloud read/mutation):** Google Compute Engine now supports `gcloud compute instances create --max-run-duration=<duration> --instance-termination-action=DELETE`, or a fixed RFC3339 `--termination-time=<timestamp>` paired with `DELETE`. Fixed termination-time is preferable for a non-restartable, owner-supervised G2 evidence run; verify the actual scheduled timestamp after create and never restart to extend it. This is a VM lifecycle safety rail, **NOT a hard all-project ₹ cost cap**. https://docs.cloud.google.com/compute/docs/instances/limit-vm-runtime ; https://docs.cloud.google.com/sdk/gcloud/reference/compute/instances/create . Boot disk must have `autoDelete=true`, no snapshots/extra disks or retained static IP; after VM auto-deletes independently confirm disks/IP/firewall/VPC cleanup. `STOP` alone does not stop disk charges. https://docs.cloud.google.com/compute/docs/instances/deleting-instance .

**Proposed single-cost-bound VM architecture, NOT EXECUTABLE OR OWNER-COST-APPROVED:**
- `oldx-staging` only, Mumbai `asia-south1` (exact available zone is **UNKNOWN**), isolated **NEW custom VPC + one subnet** (names TBD, no inherited default VPC `default-allow-ssh`), one Ubuntu on-demand `e2-standard-2` 2vCPU/8GiB, single 50 GiB `pd-standard` boot disk auto-delete, no custom image, no persistent snapshots/registry upload/GCP token/Firebase access. Guest VM has `--no-service-account` if supported with intended OS Login/IAP SSH flow; otherwise STOP rather than granting the production/staging runtime SA.
- Network: one **ephemeral, not reserved static, external IPv4** for outbound public node/BuildKit/Grype and DB downloads and later operating-system packages; **NO** public inbound SSH/HTTP. Narrow ingress TCP/22 ONLY `35.235.240.0/20` (Google IAP) to the named temporary VM; verify hierarchical/project firewall inheritance and OS Login/IAP IAM first. A custom VPC has implied deny ingress; never use the default VPC without proving existing broad rules cannot apply. IAP tunnels via internal IP even when the VM has an ephemeral external IPv4. No Cloud NAT required for this design, avoiding a separately running NAT gateway. Read docs: https://docs.cloud.google.com/vpc/docs/vpc ; https://docs.cloud.google.com/compute/docs/connect/ssh-best-practices/network-access ; https://docs.cloud.google.com/compute/docs/connect/ssh-using-iap ; https://docs.cloud.google.com/vpc/network-pricing .
- Owner's **Windows Cloud CLI already installed/authenticated** for `oldx-staging` and old Cloud Shell private small-file `D:\\OLDX` SCP and SHA256 **PASS** (Issue #17 comment 6935103541). Do not reinstall/retry Cloud Shell SSH. New VM will have a distinct host key and identity: use `gcloud compute scp --tunnel-through-iap --project=oldx-staging --zone=<verified-zone>` for one VM→Windows private transfer only **after** independent host key and access proof for the new VM. Stage archive/report/checksums under private owner-controlled new Windows run directory, compare raw file SHA256, then promote; never assume old Cloud Shell key authorizes new VM. Documentation: https://docs.cloud.google.com/sdk/gcloud/reference/compute/scp .
- Time: propose **one fixed 2-hour maximum VM lifetime**, auto-DELETE and owner manual earlier delete after verified private retention. `2h` is a planning bound, not a promised completion time. Stop and lose ephemeral evidence rather than exceed the fixed deadline or bypass protections. No VM restart/extension, no second attempt on ambiguous result; before starting any long scan/transfers confirm enough time remains.
- Scratch: require **>=14 GiB actually free** on newly prepared VM shared Docker/root filesystem, not nominal 50GiB disk capacity; retain the previous 14336MiB peak budgeting and 2.5GiB headroom. One linux/amd64 derived OCI archive; zero Cloud Run/Firebase/Registry/Cloud Build operations.
- Public example pricing, **not a firm quote**: secondary current listings show Mumbai `e2-standard-2` about USD $0.0805/VM-hour; 2h compute ≈ USD $0.161. Google lists ephemeral IPv4 USD $0.005/hour (≈ USD $0.01 for 2h, subject to account free tier); disk and network billed separately; sample network egress 3GiB × $0.12/GiB = $0.36 BEFORE free-tier/tier adjustments; sample totals before pd-standard, tax, bill-credit and any other resources ≈ **$0.531**. 50 GiB disk charge must be priced using the **Mumbai-specific selected disk SKU**, never silently substitute Iowa price, and outbound archive byte limit/transfer timing must be set. **Important correction to previous risk inference, now grounded by upstream exact Grype v0.121.3 code:** `grype/db/v6/installation/curator.go` calls unarchive, then `activate()->hydrate()->WriteImportMetadata()->replaceDB`. `hydrate()` itself only calls `mon.Set("hydrating")` (not an INFO log) and invokes `db.Hydrater()`. In exact `grype/db/v6/db.go` `Hydrater` opens SQLite writable with automatic GORM migrations and **creates/populates indexes**; it can legitimately increase the file size beyond compressed TAR header's raw `2638049280` bytes. Therefore, previous Grype temporary `vulnerability.db` of `2875367424` bytes being LARGER than archive raw entry does **NOT** by itself prove corruption or that it was stuck solely within unarchive. Source: https://github.com/anchore/grype/blob/v0.121.3/grype/db/v6/db.go and https://github.com/anchore/grype/blob/v0.121.3/grype/db/v6/installation/curator.go .

**Next shortest safe owner-side step:** validate this already-extracted native SQLite file in **read-only** mode using official `sqlite3 -readonly` + bounded `PRAGMA quick_check;` rather than doing another Grype raw import immediately. Official SQLite documentation: https://www.sqlite.org/cli.html ; https://www.sqlite.org/pragma.html . `quick_check` is not as comprehensive as `integrity_check` but is a bounded preliminary data-structure check; Grype's own full `db status valid`, freshness and hashed DB still required after subsequent separately isolated Grype raw import. Install only the Ubuntu-signed small `sqlite3` CLI package if it is absent; otherwise no install.
```bat
wsl -d OLDX-Ubuntu-24.04 --exec /bin/sh -c "set -eu; d=/root/.oldx-g2-tools/native-db-v6.1.10; f=$d/vulnerability.db; test -f $f || { echo STOP_NATIVE_DB_MISSING; exit 1; }; [ $(stat -c %s $f) -eq 2638049280 ] || { echo STOP_DB_SIZE_MISMATCH; exit 1; }; if ! command -v sqlite3 >/dev/null 2>&1; then DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends sqlite3; fi; [ ! -e $d/sqlite-quickcheck.txt ] || { echo STOP_CHECK_EXISTS; exit 1; }; echo SQLITE_QUICKCHECK_START; timeout -k 5s 180s sqlite3 -readonly $f 'PRAGMA quick_check;' > $d/sqlite-quickcheck.txt || { echo STOP_SQLITE_QUICKCHECK_COMMAND; exit 1; }; cat $d/sqlite-quickcheck.txt; grep -Fxq ok $d/sqlite-quickcheck.txt || { echo STOP_SQLITE_QUICKCHECK_NOT_OK; exit 1; }; echo SQLITE_QUICKCHECK_PASS_NO_IMPORT"
```

**Authoritative upstream OpenSSL 10 Nov 2026 advisory**, https://openssl-library.org/news/secadv/20261110.txt: the four *new* OpenSSL CVEs in this image are categorized `Low` **by upstream OpenSSL**, though exact pinned Grype database rated all four `HIGH`. Their triggering conditions differ:

- `CVE-2026-88823`: OpenSSL AES-GCM-SIV with multi-part AAD including an empty increment, or context reinitialisation, causes unauthenticated AAD.
- `CVE-2026-95691`: vulnerable **OpenSSL QUIC client**, malicious server retransmitting CRYPTO data; use-after-free.
- `CVE-2026-95692`: AES-GCM-SIV with IV/nonce length not equal to the required 12 bytes; out-of-bounds read or IV commitment weakness.

Official advisory says fixes landed in upstream source and **releases containing them are scheduled for 1 December 2026**, including intended OpenSSL 3.5.10; not yet a validated Debian 13 package fix for this immutable image. As of 18 Nov Debian tracker pages for all four still show `trixie (security) 3.5.7-1~deb13u3` vulnerable (https://security-tracker.debian.org/tracker/CVE-2026-107496 , /CVE-2026-88823 , /CVE-2026-95691 , /CVE-2026-95692). **Do NOT silently replace the OS/OpenSSL package with future/unreleased or non-Debian upstream artifacts**, mutate the pinned image or claim low upstream severity overrides Grype's report. ## 18 Nov 2026 — B1 post-test recurring-cost STOP and cleanup facts (source-only)

**Owner direction:** After reviewing the distinction between one-time B1 trial costs and residual monthly staging charges, owner answered `ok aagal vadho`. This is authorization for a **source/public-document read-only cost-and-recovery review**, not execution of any Cloud Run, Artifact Registry, Logging, Firebase, billing, IAM, R1/R2 or production mutation. GitHub review baseline HEAD prior to this note `e8d02b15d9f57fce65bf25471ad96baf5a131d43`. Historical G2 OCI/scan/runtime, G4 Analyzer and recovery preimage were NOT rerun.

**Official lifecycle facts verified 18 Nov (important budget correction):**
- [Cloud Run Manage revisions](https://docs.cloud.google.com/run/docs/managing/revisions): A revision does **not** consume resources and is **not billed when not receiving requests**, **unless** minimum-instance settings cause idle provisioned instances. Deleting a Cloud Run revision does **NOT** delete its registry image. It is **not possible to delete the latest revision**, a revision able to receive traffic or the sole revision; deleting any eligible revision is irreversible. The newly deployed B1 might be the latest revision, so do **NOT** promise or mandate revision deletion as normal cost cleanup.
- [Cloud Run minimum instances](https://docs.cloud.google.com/run/docs/configuring/min-instances): revision-level minimum instances can keep a tagged revision warm and billed even when it receives zero requests. Therefore explicitly require prospective B1 **revision min0**, service/request-based billing and **revision max1**, and verify them from actual new revision only after separate owner consent. A 0%-main tagged revision has a direct invocable URL; it is not automatically disabled.
- [Cloud Run traffic/tag rollback](https://docs.cloud.google.com/run/docs/rollouts-rollbacks-traffic-migration): R1 **remove only ownerreadv2 tag** after no-drift; this cuts the tagged access path but **does not remove the revision, revert enabled env or delete container image**. The distinct R2 can restore the pre-approved frozen disabled service template only with fresh no-drift and explicit separate owner authorization. Never run broad `--clear-tags` or traffic promotion.
- [Artifact Registry manage images](https://docs.cloud.google.com/artifact-registry/docs/docker/manage-images): Registry image storage is independent of Cloud Run revision/tag. Retained artifact versions can have recurring storage expense, subject to billing-account-wide free tier; image deletion may be irreversible/affect other retained references. **NO automatic registry delete, tag rewrite, cleanup-policy change, re-push or garbage collection** is authorized by an R1/R2 approval. The only verified new G2 artifact is still a **LOCAL** `candidate.oci.tar` and not a published registry image.

**Future B1 exit evidence (PROPOSED, NOT EXECUTED):** For the exact new tag `ownerreadv2`, immutable image digest and new provider-assigned revision ID, capture sanitized before/after receipts: (1) protected historical 100/0/0 main traffic and historic tags unchanged, (2) no new tag URL after R1, (3) exact default-OFF env flags and existing service/runtime/invoker IAM unchanged; no unexpected 200 owner/data path, (4) revision min0/max1, request billing and active instance/request telemetry as observed; no imaginary 20min auto-delete, (5) separately authorized R2/restore only when indicated by no-drift and valid frozen preimage, (6) eligible image storage/retention and billed gross **Cloud Run vs Registry vs Logging** measured from genuine scoped source where permitted, (7) G4 runtime rights stay **UNVERIFIED** under disabled-only B1 and require future separately approved synthetic tests/cleanup, (8) preserve local private OCI, scan, journal and receipts; do NOT delete to save disk without exact human approval. Stop and ask for manual recovery if no-drift, billing observation, access cut-off or receipt custody is ambiguous.

**One-time vs recurring cost judgment:** The prior **USD 0.0303 (~₹3.22 planning FX/tax)** is ONLY an illustrative continuous 20-minute 1vCPU/512Mi Cloud Run *compute scenario*, NOT the project's charge, maximum spend, or promise of zero after the rollback. Conversely, an inactive min0 untagged revision generally has no active compute cost and need not be dangerously deleted just to claim savings. Cloud Run monthly ₹100 *service-only* cap and project ₹2,000 *alerts-only* remain insufficient G8 full-service cost enforcement.
